Lemma 4.30

Let
L
be a modal predicate logic such that
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is a Skvortsov class. Then
n\in\mathbb{Y}
implies
S_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in\mathop{\mbox{{{QML}}}^{\mathit{c}}_{\mathit{wfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
4.29
with the difference that the corresponding Kripke frame should be finite; such a frame exists since
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is a Skvortsov class.

As corollaries of the lemmas, we obtain the following theorems.

Theorem 4.31

Let
L
be a modal predicate logic such that
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is a wKHC class. Then
\mathbf{QK}
and
L_{\mathit{dfin}}\oplus\bm{bf}
are recursively inseparable in the language with a single binary predicate letter, two unary predicate letters, and two individual variables.

Follows from Lemmas
4.28
and
4.29
.

Theorem 4.32

Let
L
be a modal predicate logic such that
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is a Skvortsov class. Then
\mathbf{QK}
and
L_{\mathit{wfin}}\oplus\bm{bf}
are recursively inseparable in the language with a single binary predicate letter, two unary predicate letters, and two individual variables.

Follows from Lemmas
4.28
and
4.30
.

To eliminate the binary letter
P
, we need some additional condition, similar to the condition proposed in
[
22
, Theorem 3]
. We say that a Kripke frame
\mathfrak{F}=\langle W,R\rangle
satisfies the
Kontchakov–Kurucz–Zakharyaschev condition
(for short,
KKZ
) if there are
w\in W
and two infinite disjoint subsets,
V
and
U
, of
W
such that
V\subseteq R(w)
and
U\subseteq R(v)
, for every
v\in V
.
We say that a modal predicate logic
L
is
KKZ-friendly
if there exists a Kripke frame
\mathfrak{F}\in\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
satisfying KKZ. It is known that the monadic fragments — with an infinite supply of unary predicate letters — of KKZ-friendly modal predicate logics are undecidable in the language with two individual variables
[
22
]
. For our purposes, we need a weaker condition. We say that a class
\mathscr{C}
of Kripke frames satisfies the
weak Kontchakov–Kurucz–Zakharyaschev condition
(for short,
wKKZ
) if, for every
n\in\mathds{N}
, there exist a Kripke frame
\langle W,R\rangle\in\mathscr{C}
, a world
w\in W
, and a subset
V
of
R(w)
such that
|V|\geqslant n
and, for every
v\in V
,
|R(v)\setminus V|\geqslant n
.
Clearly, if a class
\mathscr{C}
of Kripke frames contains a frame that satisfies KKZ, then it also satisfies KHC, wKHC, and wKKZ. But if
\mathscr{C}
contains only finite Kripke frames, then it can not satisfy KKZ; at the same time, the class of all finite Kripke frames satisfies wKKZ. If
\mathscr{C}
satisfies wKKZ, then it also satisfies wKHC. Nevertheless, the class of all frames of depth
17
17

17

The largest number of worlds in chains in a (transitive) Kripke frame.
two satisfies KHC and wKHC but it does not satisfy both KKZ and wKKZ.

Let us expand
S_{2}
on
\mathcal{ML}
-formulas, i.e., let
S_{2}
be a formula substitution that substitutes
\Box(Q(x_{1})\vee Q(x_{2}))
for
P(x_{1},x_{2})
in
\mathcal{ML}
-formulas.

Lemma 4.33

If
n\in\mathbb{X}
, then
S_{2}S_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\in\mathbf{QK}
.

Immediately follows from Lemma
4.28
, since
\mathbf{QK}
is closed under Substitution.

Lemma 4.34

Let
L
be a modal predicate logic such that
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is a wKKZ class. Then,
n\in\mathbb{Y}
implies

S_{2}S_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in\mathop{\mbox{{{QML}}}^{\mathit{c}}_{\mathit{dfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
.

Let
\mathfrak{M}=\langle\mathfrak{F}\odot\mathcal{D}^{\prime},I\rangle
be the model constructed in the proof of Lemma
4.29
. Since
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is a wKKZ class, it contains a Kripke frame
\bar{\mathfrak{F}}=\langle\bar{W},\bar{R}\rangle
such that there exist
\bar{w}_{0}\in W
and a subset
V
of
\bar{R}(\bar{w}_{0})
such that
|V|\geqslant 2|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}|^{3}
and, for every
v\in V
,
|\bar{R}(v)\setminus V|\geqslant 2|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}|^{3}
.

Recall that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
contains
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{D}=\{0,\ldots,r+4\}\times\{0,\ldots,r+4\}
.
Let
V^{\prime}=\{\bar{w}_{a}\in V:a\in\mathcal{D}\}
, where
\bar{w}_{a}=\bar{w}_{b}
if, and only if,
a=b
. For every
v\in\{\bar{w}_{0}\}\cup V
, choose a set

\begin{array}[]{lcll}U(v)&=&\{u^{v}_{bc}\in\bar{R}(v):b,c\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\},\end{array}

so that, for all
v,v^{\prime}\in\{\bar{w}_{0}\}\cup V
and all
b,c,d,e\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}u^{v}_{bc}=u^{v^{\prime}}_{de}&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\{b,c\}=\{d,e\};\end{array}

notice that it is possible, since the sets
V
and
\bar{R}(v)\setminus V
, for every
v\in V
, are quite big.
Next, define a model
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}=\langle\bar{\mathfrak{F}}\odot\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\bar{I}\rangle
so that, for every
w\in\bar{W}
and every
b\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\bar{\mathfrak{M}},w\models G(b)&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&b\in\mathcal{D};\\
\bar{\mathfrak{M}},w\models C(b)&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\mbox{for some $a\in\mathcal{D}$, both $w=\bar{w}_{a}$ and $b=a$};\\
\bar{\mathfrak{M}},w\not\models Q(b)&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\mbox{for some $v\in\{\bar{w}_{0}\}\cup V$ and some $c\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$,}\\
&&\mbox{both $w\in\{u^{v}_{bc},u^{v}_{cb}\}$ and $\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\models P(b,c)$,}\end{array}

where
\mathfrak{M}
and
w_{0}
are defined in the proof of Lemma
4.29
.

Let us compare
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}
and
\mathfrak{M}
. Observe that, for every
a\in\mathcal{D}\cup\{0\}
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\{{w}\in{W}:{\mathfrak{M}},{w}\models C(a)\}&=&\{{w}_{a}\};\\
\{{w}\in\bar{W}:\bar{\mathfrak{M}},{w}\models C(a)\}&=&\{\bar{w}_{a}\};\end{array}

also, for every
w\in\{w_{0}\}\cup R(w_{0})
, every
\bar{w}\in\{\bar{w}_{0}\}\cup R(\bar{w}_{0})
, and all
b,c\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},{w}\models G(b)&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\bar{\mathfrak{M}},\bar{w}\models G(b);\\
\mathfrak{M},{w}\models P(b,c)&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\bar{\mathfrak{M}},\bar{w}\models\Box(Q(b)\vee Q(c)).\end{array}

Since
\mathfrak{M},{w}_{0}\not\models S_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
, we readily obtain that
\bar{\mathfrak{M}},\bar{w}_{0}\not\models S_{2}S_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
.

Thus,
S_{2}S_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in\mathop{\mbox{{{QML}}}^{\mathit{c}}_{\mathit{dfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
.

Lemma 4.35

Let
L
be a modal predicate logic such that
\mathop{\mbox{{{fin}}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is a wKKZ class. Then
n\in\mathbb{Y}
implies
S_{2}S_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in\mathop{\mbox{{{QML}}}^{\mathit{c}}_{\mathit{wfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
4.34
with the difference that the corresponding Kripke frame should be finite; such a frame exists since
\mathop{\mbox{{{fin}}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is a wKKZ class.

As corollaries of the lemmas, we obtain the following theorems.

Theorem 4.36

Let
L
be a modal predicate logic such that
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is a wKKZ class. Then
\mathbf{QK}
and
L_{\mathit{dfin}}\oplus\bm{bf}
are recursively inseparable in the language with three unary predicate letters and two individual variables.

Follows from Lemmas
4.33
and
4.34
.

Theorem 4.37

Let
L
be a modal predicate logic such that
\mathop{\mbox{{{fin}}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is a wKKZ class. Then
\mathbf{QK}
and
L_{\mathit{wfin}}\oplus\bm{bf}
are recursively inseparable in the language with three unary predicate letters and two individual variables.

Follows from Lemmas
4.33
and
4.35
.

Theorems
4.36
and
4.37
provide us with the following corollary on particular logics.

Corollary 4.38

Let
L
be one of
\mathbf{QK}
,
\mathbf{QT}
,
\mathbf{QKB}
,
\mathbf{QKTB}
,
\mathbf{QK4}
,
\mathbf{QS4}
,
\mathbf{QK5}
,
\mathbf{QS5}
,
\mathbf{QK45}
,
\mathbf{QK4B}
,
\mathbf{QGL}
,
\mathbf{QGrz}
,
\mathbf{QwGrz}
,
\mathbf{QK4}\oplus\bm{bd}_{m}
,
\mathbf{QS4}\oplus\bm{bd}_{m}
,
\mathbf{QGL}\oplus\bm{bd}_{m}
,
\mathbf{QGrz}\oplus\bm{bd}_{m}
,
\mathbf{QwGrz}\oplus\bm{bd}_{m}
, where
m\geqslant 3
. Then
L
and
L_{\mathit{dfin}}
as well as
L
and
L_{\mathit{wfin}}
are recursively inseparable in the language with three unary predicate letters and two individual variables.

Now, we are going to eliminate all predicate letters except for a single unary one. To this end, we propose some other relativizations and substitutions. Also, we need classes of Kripke frames to satisfy a slightly stronger condition than wKKZ.
We will not explicitly formulate this condition. Instead, we will describe the logics whose classes of Kripke frames satisfy it. However, our description will allow readers to adjust the resulting construction, including both formulas and additional requirements for classes of frames. Additionally, some of the technical statements below will involve subframe modal predicate logics. Note that the class of Kripke frames of such logic satisfies wKHC if, and only if, it is a Skvortsov class.

First, let us define a modification
S^{\prime}_{3}
of
S_{3}
. Let
S^{\prime}_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
be a formula obtained from
\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
by applying
S_{3}
to it and then replacing every occurrence of
C(x)
and
C(y)
with
\mathit{tile}^{\prime}_{k_{n}+9}(x)
and
\mathit{tile}^{\prime}_{k_{n}+9}(y)
, respectively.

Let
p
be a proposition letter. Define
\Box_{p}
by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Box_{p}\varphi&=&\Box(p\to\varphi).\end{array}

For an
\mathcal{ML}
-formula
\varphi
, let
\varphi_{p}
be the formula obtained from
\varphi
by replacing every occurrence of
\Box
with
\Box_{p}
.

Lemma 4.39

If
n\in\mathbb{X}
, then
p\to(S^{\prime}_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}})_{p}\in\mathbf{QK}
.

We give just a sketch of the proof.

Let
n\in\mathbb{X}
. Then, by Lemma
4.27
,
\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\in\mathbf{QK}
. Then,
S^{\prime}_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\in\mathbf{QK}
, since
\mathbf{QK}
is closed under Substitution.

Suppose that
p\to(S^{\prime}_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}})_{p}\not\in\mathbf{QK}
. Then there exists a model that refutes the formula.
If we remove all the worlds at which
p
is not true from the model, then we obtain a model refuting
S^{\prime}_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
, which gives us a contradiction.
Thus,
p\to(S^{\prime}_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}})_{p}\in\mathbf{QK}
.

Lemma 4.40

Let
L
be a modal predicate logic such that
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is a wKHC class. Then
n\in\mathbb{Y}
implies
p\to(S^{\prime}_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}})_{p}\not\in\mathop{\mbox{{{QML}}}^{\mathit{c}}_{\mathit{dfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
.

We are going to apply an argumentation similar to that used in the proof of Lemma
4.29
; we do not need to simulate
G
again, but we do need to simulate
C
as well.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Y}
.
Then, by Lemma
3.10
,
\mathit{MTiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in\mathbf{QCl}_{\mathit{fin}}\uplus\bm{sib}
. Hence,
there exists a finite classical model
\bm{M}=\langle\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}\rangle
such that
\bm{M}\models\bm{sib}
and
\bm{M}\not\models\mathit{MTiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
; we may assume that
\bm{M}
is the model defined in the proof of Lemma
3.10
, in particular,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{D}&=&\{0,\ldots,r+4\}\times\{0,\ldots,r+4\},\\
\end{array}

for a suitable
r\in\mathds{N}
.

For every
a\in\mathcal{D}
, take the elements

e_{0}^{a},\ldots,e_{k_{n}+11}^{a}
and
e_{P}^{a},e_{R}^{a},e_{U}^{a},e_{C}^{a}
; let

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}&=&\mathcal{D}\cup\{e_{0}^{a},\ldots,e_{k_{n}+11}^{a},e_{P}^{a},e_{R}^{a},e_{U}^{a},e_{C}^{a}:a\in\mathcal{D}\}.\end{array}

Let
\mathfrak{F}=\langle W,R\rangle
be a Kripke frame of
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
and
w_{0}
a world of
W
such that
|R(w_{0})\setminus\{w_{0}\}|\geqslant|\mathcal{D}|
; such frame exists since
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is a wKHC class. Then
R(w_{0})\setminus\{w_{0}\}
contains a subset
W^{\prime}=\{w_{a}:a\in\mathcal{D}\}
, where
w_{a}=w_{b}
if, and only if,
a=b
.
Let
\mathfrak{M}=\langle\mathfrak{F}\odot\mathcal{D}^{\prime},I\rangle
be a Kripke model such that

•

for every
w\in W
and every
a\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},w\models p&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&w\in\{w_{0}\}\cup W^{\prime};\\
\mathfrak{M},w\models G(a)&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\mbox{$a\in\mathcal{D}$ and $\mathfrak{M},w\models p$;}\\
\end{array}

•

I(w_{0},P)
is the symmetric closure of the relation

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{I}(P)\cup\{\langle a,e_{0}^{a}\rangle,\langle e_{0}^{a},e_{1}^{a}\rangle,\ldots,\langle e_{k_{n}+10}^{a},e_{k_{n}+11}^{a}\rangle:a\in\mathcal{D}\}\\
\phantom{\mathcal{I}(P)}\cup\{\langle e_{m}^{a},e_{P}^{a}\rangle:\mbox{$m\in\{0,\ldots,k_{n}\}$ and $\bm{M}\models P_{m}(a)$}\}\\
\phantom{\mathcal{I}(P)}\cup\{\langle e_{k_{n}+i}^{a},e_{R}^{a}\rangle:\mbox{$i\in\{1,2,3,4\}$ and $\bm{M}\models R_{i}(a)$}\}\\
\phantom{\mathcal{I}(P)}\cup\{\langle e_{k_{n}+j+4}^{a},e_{U}^{a}\rangle:\mbox{$j\in\{1,2,3,4\}$ and $\bm{M}\models U_{j}(a)$}\};\end{array}

•

for every
a\in\mathcal{D}
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}I(w_{a},P)&=&I(w_{0},P)\cup\{\langle e_{k_{n}+9}^{a},e_{C}^{a}\rangle,\langle e_{C}^{a},e_{k_{n}+9}^{a}\rangle\};\end{array}

•

for every
w\in W\setminus(W^{\prime}\cup\{w_{0}\})
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}I(w,P)&=&\varnothing;\end{array}

see Figure
17
for a part of
I(w_{a},P)
.
Then it is not hard to show by routine verification that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\models p\to(S^{\prime}_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}})_{p}
; we leave the details to the reader.

Thus,
p\to(S^{\prime}_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}})_{p}\not\in\mathop{\mbox{{{QML}}}^{\mathit{c}}_{\mathit{dfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
.

a
e_{m}^{a}
e_{k_{n}+i}^{a}
e_{k_{n}+j+4}^{a}
e_{k_{n}+9}^{a}
e_{k_{n}+10}^{a}
e_{k_{n}+11}^{a}
e_{P}^{a}
e_{R}^{a}
e_{U}^{a}
e_{C}^{a}
b
c
e_{k_{n}+11}^{b}
e_{k_{n}+11}^{c}
e_{C}^{b}
e_{C}^{c}

Figure 17:
Simulation of
P_{m}(a)
,
R_{i}(a)
,
U_{j}(a)
,
C(a)
,
\neg C(b)
, and
\neg C(c)

Let
q
and
r
be two proposition letters different from
p
.
Let
S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
be a formula obtained from
p\to(S^{\prime}_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}})_{p}
by substituting
\Box(q\to Q(x_{1})\vee Q(x_{2}))
and
\Box(r\to Q(x_{1}))
for
P(x_{1},x_{2})
and
G(x_{1})
, respectively.

Lemma 4.41

If
n\in\mathbb{X}
, then
S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\in\mathbf{QK}
.

Follows from Lemma
4.40
, since
\mathbf{QK}
is closed under Substitution.

Lemma 4.42

If
n\in\mathbb{Y}
, then

S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in L_{\mathit{wfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{3}\oplus\bm{bf}
for
L\in\{\mathbf{QGL},\mathbf{QGrz}\}
.

Let
L=\mathbf{QGL}
.
Let
n\in\mathbb{Y}
.
Let
\mathfrak{M}=\langle\mathfrak{F}\odot\mathcal{D}^{\prime},I\rangle
be the model constructed in the proof of Lemma
4.40
.
Recall that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
contains
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{D}=\{0,\ldots,r+4\}\times\{0,\ldots,r+4\}
.
Define

\begin{array}[]{lcl}W_{0}&=&\{\bar{w}_{0}\};\\
W_{1}&=&\{\bar{w}_{a}:a\in\mathcal{D}\};\\
W_{2}^{P}&=&\{u^{a}_{bc}:\mbox{$a\in\mathcal{D}$, $b,c\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$}\};\\
W_{2}^{G}&=&\{u_{G}\};\\
\bar{W}&=&W_{0}\cup W_{1}\cup W_{2}^{P}\cup W_{2}^{G}.\end{array}

Let
\bar{R}
be the transitive closure of the following relation on
W
:

\begin{array}[]{c}\displaystyle(W_{0}\times W_{1})\cup\{\langle\bar{w}_{a},u^{a}_{bc}\rangle:\mbox{$a\in\mathcal{D}$, $b,c\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$}\}\cup(W_{1}\times W_{2}^{G}).\end{array}

Let
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}=\langle\bar{\mathfrak{F}}\odot\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\bar{I}\rangle
be a model such that, for every
w\in\bar{W}
, every
a\in\mathcal{D}
, and all
b,c,d\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\bar{\mathfrak{M}},w\models p&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&w\in W_{0}\cup W_{1};\\
\bar{\mathfrak{M}},w\models q&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&w\in W_{2}^{P};\\
\bar{\mathfrak{M}},w\models r&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&w=u_{G};\\
\bar{\mathfrak{M}},u_{G}\models Q(d)&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&d\in\mathcal{D};\\
\bar{\mathfrak{M}},u^{a}_{bc}\not\models Q(d)&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\mbox{$d\in\{b,c\}$ and $\mathfrak{M},w_{a}\not\models P(b,c)$.}\\
\end{array}

Let us compare
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}
and
\mathfrak{M}
.
Observe that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\{{w}\in{W}:{\mathfrak{M}},{w}\models p\}&=&\{w_{0}\}\cup\{{w}_{a}:a\in\mathcal{D}\};\\
\{{w}\in\bar{W}:\bar{\mathfrak{M}},{w}\models p\}&=&\{\bar{w}_{0}\}\cup\{\bar{w}_{a}:a\in\mathcal{D}\};\end{array}

also, for every
a\in\mathcal{D}\cup\{0\}
and all
b,c\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},{w}_{a}\models G(b)&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\bar{\mathfrak{M}},\bar{w}_{a}\models\Box(r\to Q(b));\\
\mathfrak{M},{w}_{a}\models P(b,c)&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\bar{\mathfrak{M}},\bar{w}_{a}\models\Box(q\to Q(b)\vee Q(c)).\end{array}

Since
\mathfrak{M},{w}_{0}\not\models p\to(S^{\prime}_{3}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}})_{p}
, we readily obtain that
\bar{\mathfrak{M}},\bar{w}_{0}\not\models S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
.
Thus,
S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in\mathbf{QGL}_{\mathit{wfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{3}\oplus\bm{bf}
.

Let
L=\mathbf{QGrz}
.
Then just take the model obtained from
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}
by replacing
\bar{R}
with its reflexive closure; we leave the details to the reader.

Let us eliminate
p
,
q
, and
r
. There are many ways to do this with formulas that do not contain predicate letters different from
Q
. We show two of them, leading to slightly different results.

Let
S_{5}
be a formula substitution defined by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}S_{5}(p)&=&\Diamond\forall x\,Q(x)\wedge\Diamond\forall x\,\neg Q(x);\\
S_{5}(q)&=&\exists x\,Q(x)\wedge\Diamond\forall x\,\neg Q(x)\wedge\neg\Diamond\forall x\,Q(x);\\
S_{5}(r)&=&\exists x\,\neg Q(x)\wedge\Diamond\forall x\,Q(x)\wedge\neg\Diamond\forall x\,\neg Q(x).\end{array}

Lemma 4.43

If
n\in\mathbb{X}
, then
S_{5}S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\in\mathbf{QK}
.

Follows from Lemma
4.41
, since
\mathbf{QK}
is closed under Substitution.

Lemma 4.44

If
n\in\mathbb{Y}
, then

S_{5}S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in L_{\mathit{wfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{4}\oplus\bm{bf}
for
L\in\{\mathbf{QGL},\mathbf{QGrz}\}
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Y}
and let
L=\mathbf{QGL}
. We modify the model
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}=\langle\bar{\mathfrak{F}}\odot\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\bar{I}\rangle
defined in the proof of Lemma
4.42
and based on the Kripke frame
\bar{\mathfrak{F}}=\langle\bar{W},\bar{R}\rangle
. Let

\begin{array}[]{lcl}W_{3}&=&\{v^{-},v^{+}\};\\
\tilde{W}&=&\bar{W}\cup W_{3};\\
\end{array}

let also
\tilde{R}
be the transitive closure of the relation

\begin{array}[]{c}\bar{R}\cup(W_{2}^{P}\times\{v^{-}\})\cup(W_{2}^{G}\times\{v^{+}\}).\end{array}

Let
\tilde{\mathfrak{F}}=\langle\tilde{W},\tilde{R}\rangle
and let
\tilde{\mathfrak{M}}=\langle\tilde{\mathfrak{F}}\odot\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\tilde{I}\rangle
be a model such that, for every
w\in\bar{W}
and every
d\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\tilde{\mathfrak{M}},w\models Q(d)&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\bar{M},w\models Q(d)\\
\end{array}

and also

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\tilde{\mathfrak{M}},v^{-}\models\forall x\,\neg Q(x)&\mbox{and}&\tilde{\mathfrak{M}},v^{+}\models\forall x\,Q(x).\end{array}

Then it is not hard to see that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\{w\in\tilde{W}:\tilde{\mathfrak{M}},w\models S_{5}(p)\}&=&\{w\in\bar{W}:\bar{\mathfrak{M}},w\models p\};\\
\{w\in\tilde{W}:\tilde{\mathfrak{M}},w\models S_{5}(q)\}&=&\{w\in\bar{W}:\bar{\mathfrak{M}},w\models q\};\\
\{w\in\tilde{W}:\tilde{\mathfrak{M}},w\models S_{5}(r)\}&=&\{w\in\bar{W}:\bar{\mathfrak{M}},w\models r\}.\\
\end{array}

Since
\bar{\mathfrak{M}},\bar{w}_{0}\not\models S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
, we readily obtain that
\tilde{\mathfrak{M}},\bar{w}_{0}\not\models S_{5}S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
.
Thus,
S_{5}S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in\mathbf{QGL}_{\mathit{wfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{4}\oplus\bm{bf}
.

Let
L=\mathbf{QGrz}
.
Then just take the model obtained from
\tilde{\mathfrak{M}}
by replacing
\tilde{R}
with its reflexive closure; we leave the details to the reader.

Lemma 4.45

If
n\in\mathbb{Y}
, then

S_{5}S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in L_{\mathit{dfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{4}\oplus\bm{bf}
for
L\in\{\mathbf{QGL},\mathbf{QGrz}\}
.

Both
\mathbf{QGL}
and
\mathbf{QGrz}
are subframe logics; therefore, the statement follows from Lemma
4.44
.

Theorem 4.46

Logics
\mathbf{QK}
and
\mathbf{QGL}_{\mathit{wfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{4}\oplus\bm{bf}
as well as
\mathbf{QK}
and
\mathbf{QGrz}_{\mathit{wfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{4}\oplus\bm{bf}
are recursively inseparable in the language with a single unary predicate letter and two individual variables.

Follows from Lemmas
4.43
and
4.44
.

Theorem 4.47

Logics
\mathbf{QK}
and
\mathbf{QGL}_{\mathit{dfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{4}\oplus\bm{bf}
as well as
\mathbf{QK}
and
\mathbf{QGrz}_{\mathit{dfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{4}\oplus\bm{bf}
are recursively inseparable in the language with a single unary predicate letter and two individual variables.

Follows from Lemmas
4.43
and
4.45
.

Corollary 4.48

Let
\mathbf{QK}\subseteq L\subseteq L^{\prime}
and also either
L^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbf{QGL}\oplus\bm{bd}_{4}\oplus\bm{bf}
or
L^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbf{QGrz}\oplus\bm{bd}_{4}\oplus\bm{bf}
. Then
L
and
L^{\prime}_{\mathit{wfin}}
as well as
L
and
L^{\prime}_{\mathit{dfin}}
are recursively inseparable in the language with a single unary predicate letter and two individual variables.

Corollary 4.49

Let
L
be one of
\mathbf{QK}
,
\mathbf{QT}
,
\mathbf{QD}
,
\mathbf{QK4}
,
\mathbf{QD4}
,
\mathbf{QS4}
,
\mathbf{QGL}
,
\mathbf{QGrz}
,
\mathbf{QK4}\oplus\bm{bd}_{m}
,
\mathbf{QS4}\oplus\bm{bd}_{m}
,
\mathbf{QGL}\oplus\bm{bd}_{m}
,
\mathbf{QGrz}\oplus\bm{bd}_{m}
, where
m\geqslant 4
. Then
L
and
L_{\mathit{wfin}}
as well as
L
and
L_{\mathit{dfin}}
are recursively inseparable in the language with a single unary predicate letter and two individual variables.

Let
S^{\prime}_{5}
be a formula substitution defined by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}S^{\prime}_{5}(p)&=&\Diamond\Diamond\top\wedge\Diamond\Box\bot;\\
S^{\prime}_{5}(q)&=&\Box\bot;\\
S^{\prime}_{5}(r)&=&\Diamond\top\wedge\neg\Diamond\Box\bot.\end{array}

Lemma 4.50

If
n\in\mathbb{X}
, then
S^{\prime}_{5}S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\in\mathbf{QK}
.

Follows from Lemma
4.41
, since
\mathbf{QK}
is closed under Substitution.

Lemma 4.51

If
n\in\mathbb{Y}
, then

S^{\prime}_{5}S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in\mathbf{QwGrz}_{\mathit{wfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{3}\oplus\bm{bf}
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Y}
. Just modify the model
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}=\langle\bar{\mathfrak{F}}\odot\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\bar{I}\rangle
defined in the proof of Lemma
4.42
and based on the Kripke frame
\bar{\mathfrak{F}}=\langle\bar{W},\bar{R}\rangle
by replacing
\bar{R}
with

\begin{array}[]{c}\bar{R}\cup\{\langle u_{G},u_{G}\rangle\}.\end{array}

Let
\tilde{\mathfrak{M}}
be the resulting model.
Then it should be clear that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\{w\in\bar{W}:\tilde{\mathfrak{M}},w\models S^{\prime}_{5}(p)\}&=&\{w\in\bar{W}:\bar{\mathfrak{M}},w\models p\};\\
\{w\in\bar{W}:\tilde{\mathfrak{M}},w\models S^{\prime}_{5}(q)\}&=&\{w\in\bar{W}:\bar{\mathfrak{M}},w\models q\};\\
\{w\in\bar{W}:\tilde{\mathfrak{M}},w\models S^{\prime}_{5}(r)\}&=&\{w\in\bar{W}:\bar{\mathfrak{M}},w\models r\}.\\
\end{array}

Since
\bar{\mathfrak{M}},\bar{w}_{0}\not\models S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
, we readily obtain that
\tilde{\mathfrak{M}},\bar{w}_{0}\not\models S^{\prime}_{5}S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
.
Thus,
S^{\prime}_{5}S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in\mathbf{QwGrz}_{\mathit{wfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{3}\oplus\bm{bf}
.

Lemma 4.52

If
n\in\mathbb{Y}
, then

S^{\prime}_{5}S_{4}\mathit{M}^{\Box}_{G}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in\mathbf{QwGrz}_{\mathit{dfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{3}\oplus\bm{bf}
.

The statement follows from Lemma
4.51
, since
\mathbf{QwGrz}
is a subframe logic.

Theorem 4.53

Logics
\mathbf{QK}
and
\mathbf{QwGrz}_{\mathit{wfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{3}\oplus\bm{bf}
are recursively inseparable in the language with a single unary predicate letter and two individual variables.

Follows from Lemmas
4.50
and
4.51
.

Theorem 4.54

Logics
\mathbf{QK}
and
\mathbf{QwGrz}_{\mathit{dfin}}\oplus\bm{bd}_{3}\oplus\bm{bf}
are recursively inseparable in the language with a single unary predicate letter and two individual variables.

Follows from Lemmas
4.50
and
4.52
.

Corollary 4.55

Let
\mathbf{QK}\subseteq L\subseteq L^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbf{QwGrz}\oplus\bm{bd}_{3}\oplus\bm{bf}
. Then
L
and
L^{\prime}_{\mathit{wfin}}
as well as
L
and
L^{\prime}_{\mathit{dfin}}
are recursively inseparable in the language with a single unary predicate letter and two individual variables.

Corollary 4.56

Let
L
be one of
\mathbf{QK}
,
\mathbf{QK4}
,
\mathbf{QwGrz}
,
\mathbf{QK4}\oplus\bm{bd}_{m}
,
\mathbf{QwGrz}\oplus\bm{bd}_{m}
, where
m\geqslant 3
. Then
L
and
L_{\mathit{wfin}}
as well as
L
and
L_{\mathit{dfin}}
are recursively inseparable in the language with a single unary predicate letter and two individual variables.

5
Superintuitionistic predicate logics

5.1
Syntax and semantics

The intuitionistic predicate language is identical to the language
\mathcal{L}
defined above for both classical and superclassical logics and theories.

An
intuitionistic Kripke frame
is a Kripke frame
\mathfrak{F}=\langle W,R\rangle
where
R
is a partial order — i.e., a reflexive, transitive, and antisymmetric binary relation — on
W
. An
intuitionistic augmented frame
is an e-augmented frame
\mathfrak{F}_{D}=\langle\mathfrak{F},D\rangle
such that
\mathfrak{F}
is an intuitionistic Kripke frame. An
intuitionistic predicate Kripke model
is a predicate Kripke model
\mathfrak{M}=\langle W,R,D,I\rangle
where
\langle W,R,D\rangle
is an
intuitionistic augmented frame and the interpretation
I
satisfies the
heredity condition
: for all
w,w^{\prime}\in W
and every predicate letter
P
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}wRw^{\prime}&\Longrightarrow&I(w,P)\subseteq I(w^{\prime},P).\end{array}

The truth of an
\mathcal{L}
-formula
\varphi
at a world
w
of an intuitionistic model
\mathfrak{M}=\langle W,R,D,I\rangle
under an assignment
g
is defined recursively:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}P(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})&\leftrightharpoons&\parbox{234.76688pt}{$\langle g(x_{1}),\ldots,g(x_{n})\rangle\in P^{I,w}$,}\\
\end{array}

where
P
is an
n
-ary predicate letter;

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\parbox{84.4219pt}{{}\hfill\parbox{61.53061pt}{$\mathfrak{M},w\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\bot;$}}\\
\parbox{84.4219pt}{{}\hfill\parbox{61.53061pt}{$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\varphi^{\prime}\wedge\varphi^{\prime\prime}$}}&\leftrightharpoons&\parbox[t]{112.06136pt}{$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\varphi^{\prime}$ and $\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\varphi^{\prime\prime}$;}\\
\parbox{84.4219pt}{{}\hfill\parbox{61.53061pt}{$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\varphi^{\prime}\vee\varphi^{\prime\prime}$}}&\leftrightharpoons&\parbox[t]{112.06136pt}{$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\varphi^{\prime}$\hfill or\hfill$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\varphi^{\prime\prime}$;}\\
\parbox{84.4219pt}{{}\hfill\parbox{61.53061pt}{$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\varphi^{\prime}\to\varphi^{\prime\prime}$}}&\leftrightharpoons&\parbox[t]{234.76688pt}{$\mathfrak{M},\parbox{7.42825pt}{$v$}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\varphi^{\prime}$ implies $\mathfrak{M},v\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\varphi^{\prime\prime}$, for every $v\in R(w)$;}\\
\parbox{84.4219pt}{{}\hfill\parbox{61.53061pt}{$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\forall x\,\varphi^{\prime}$}}&\leftrightharpoons&\parbox{234.76688pt}{$\mathfrak{M},\parbox{7.42825pt}{$v$}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{h}\varphi^{\prime}$, for every $v\in R(w)$ and every assignment}\\
&&\mbox{\phantom{$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g^{\prime}}\varphi^{\prime}$, }$h$ such that $h\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x}}{{=}}g$ and $h(x)\in D_{w}$;}\\
\parbox{84.4219pt}{{}\hfill\parbox{61.53061pt}{$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\exists x\,\varphi^{\prime}$}}&\leftrightharpoons&\parbox{234.76688pt}{$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{h}\varphi^{\prime}$, for some assignment $h$ such that $h\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x}}{{=}}g$}\\
&&\mbox{\phantom{$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g^{\prime}}\varphi^{\prime}$, }and $h(x)\in D_{w}$.}\end{array}

Let
\mathfrak{M}
,
\mathfrak{F}_{D}
,
\mathfrak{F}
, and
\mathscr{C}
be an intuitionistic Kripke model, an intuitionistic augmented frame, an intuitionistic Kripke frame, and a class of intuitionistic augmented frames, respectively,
w
a world of
\mathfrak{M}
, and
\varphi
a formula with free variables
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
; then define

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\parbox{84.4219pt}{{}\hfill$\mathfrak{M}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\varphi$}&\leftrightharpoons&\parbox[t]{234.76688pt}{$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\varphi$, for every world $w$ of $\mathfrak{M}$ and every\penalty\ $g$}\\
&&\mbox{\phantom{$\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\varphi$, }such that $g(x_{1}),\ldots,g(x_{n})\in D_{w}$;}\\
\parbox{84.4219pt}{{}\hfill$\mathfrak{F}_{D}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\varphi$}&\leftrightharpoons&\parbox[t]{234.76688pt}{$\parbox{13.78157pt}{$\mathfrak{M}$}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\varphi$, for every intuitionistic model $\mathfrak{M}$ based on $\mathfrak{F}_{D}$;}\\
\parbox{84.4219pt}{{}\hfill$\mathfrak{F}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\varphi$}&\leftrightharpoons&\parbox[t]{234.76688pt}{$\parbox{13.78157pt}{$\mathfrak{M}$}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\varphi$, for every intuitionistic model $\mathfrak{M}$ based on $\mathfrak{F}$;}\\
\parbox{84.4219pt}{{}\hfill$\mathscr{C}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\varphi$}&\leftrightharpoons&\parbox[t]{191.94676pt}{$\parbox{13.78157pt}{$\mathfrak{F}_{D}$}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\varphi$, for every $\mathfrak{F}_{D}\in\mathscr{C}$.}\\
\end{array}

If
\mathfrak{S}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\varphi
, for a structure
\mathfrak{S}
(a model, a frame, etc.), we say that the formula
\varphi
is
true
, or
valid
, in (on, at)
\mathfrak{S}
; otherwise,
\varphi
is
refuted
in (on, at)
\mathfrak{S}
.
These notions, and the corresponding notations, can be extended to sets of formulas in a natural way: for a set of formulas
X
, define
\mathfrak{S}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}X
as
\mathfrak{S}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\varphi
, for every
\varphi\in X
.

The intuitionistic predicate logic
\mathbf{QInt}
is the set of
\mathcal{L}
-formulas valid on every intuitionistic Kripke frame; notice that
\mathbf{QInt}
can also be defined through a Hilbert-style calculus with a finite set of axioms
[
12
,
65
]
. A
superintuitionistic predicate logic
is a set of
\mathcal{L}
-formulas that includes
\mathbf{QInt}
and is closed under Modus Ponens, Substitution, and Generalization. If
L
is a superintuitionistic predicate logic and
\Gamma
is a set of
\mathcal{L}
-formulas, then
L+\Gamma
denotes the smallest superintuitionistic logic containing
L\cup\Gamma
. If
L
is a propositional superintuitionistic logic, then define
\mathbf{Q}L
by
\mathbf{Q}L=\mathbf{QInt}+L
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a class of intuitionistic augmented frames. Define the
superintuitionistic predicate logic
\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}}\mathscr{C}
of the class
\mathscr{C}
by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}}\mathscr{C}&=&\{\varphi\in\mathcal{L}:\mathscr{C}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\varphi\}.\end{array}

For
\alpha\in\{\mathit{c},\mathit{e}\}
and
\beta\in\{\mathit{all},\mathit{dfin},\mathit{wfin}\}
, define the logic
\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{\alpha}}_{\mathit{\beta}}}\mathscr{C}
by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{\alpha}}_{\mathit{\beta}}}\mathscr{C}&=&\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{aug}}}^{\mathit{\alpha}}_{\mathit{\beta}}}\mathscr{C}.\end{array}

For an intuitionistic Kripke frame
\mathfrak{F}
, we write
\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{\alpha}}_{\mathit{\beta}}}\mathfrak{F}
rather than
\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{\alpha}}_{\mathit{\beta}}}\{\mathfrak{F}\}
.
It should be clear that

\begin{array}[]{lclcl}\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{e}}_{\mathit{\beta}}}\mathscr{C}\subseteq\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{c}}_{\mathit{\beta}}}\mathscr{C},&&\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{\alpha}}_{\mathit{\mathit{all}}}}\mathscr{C}\subseteq\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{\alpha}}_{\mathit{\mathit{dfin}}}}\mathscr{C},&&\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{\alpha}}_{\mathit{\mathit{all}}}}\mathscr{C}\subseteq\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{\alpha}}_{\mathit{\mathit{wfin}}}}\mathscr{C}.\end{array}

For a superintuitionistic predicate logic
L
, denote by
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
the class of intuitionistic Kripke frames validating
L
.
Define

\begin{array}[]{lcl}L_{\mathit{dfin}}&=&\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{e}}_{\mathit{dfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L;\\
L_{\mathit{wfin}}&=&\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{e}}_{\mathit{wfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L.\\
\end{array}

Let
Q
be a unary predicate letter and
q
a proposition letter; the formula
\bm{cd}=\forall x\,(Q(x)\vee q)\to\forall x\,Q(x)\vee q
is valid on an intuitionistic augmented frame
\mathfrak{F}_{D}
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{F}_{D}
satisfies
(\mathit{LCD})
.
Recall also that
\mathbf{QKC}=\mathbf{QInt}+\neg p\vee\neg\neg p
.

5.2
Positive monadic fragments and three variables

Let us construct an embedding of the classical predicate logic and some classical theories into the positive fragments of
\mathbf{QInt}
and some extensions of
\mathbf{QInt}
. In view of Theorem
3.33
, to define the embedding, we shall consider the positive fragment of
\mathcal{L}
with a single binary predicate letter
P
and three individual variables
x
,
y
, and
z
. Denote by
K
the Kolmogorov translation
[
21
]
18
18

18

For our purposes, an another translation can be chosen; see
[
10
]
.
defined for formulas of the fragment as follows (we do not need the first clause for positive formulas):

\begin{array}[]{lcll}K(\bot)&=&\bot;\\
K(P(x_{1},x_{2}))&=&\neg\neg P(x_{1},x_{2}),&\mbox{where $x_{1},x_{2}\in\{x,y,z\}$};\\
K(\varphi^{\prime}\wedge\varphi^{\prime\prime})&=&\neg\neg(K(\varphi^{\prime})\wedge K(\varphi^{\prime\prime}));\\
K(\varphi^{\prime}\vee\varphi^{\prime\prime})&=&\neg\neg(K(\varphi^{\prime})\vee K(\varphi^{\prime\prime}));\\
K(\varphi^{\prime}\to\varphi^{\prime\prime})&=&\neg\neg(K(\varphi^{\prime})\to K(\varphi^{\prime\prime}));\\
K(\forall x\,\varphi^{\prime})&=&\neg\neg\forall x\,K(\varphi^{\prime});\\
K(\exists x\,\varphi^{\prime})&=&\neg\neg\exists x\,K(\varphi^{\prime}).\\
\end{array}

It is follows from
[
21
]
that, for every formula
\varphi
of the fragment,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\varphi\in\mathbf{QCl}&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&K(\varphi)\in\mathbf{QInt}.\end{array}

(5.1)

Next, let us eliminate
\bot
by replacing it with a proposition letter
p
. To this end, for an
\mathcal{L}
-formula
\psi
, define
\psi^{p}
to be the formula obtained from
\psi
by replacing every occurrence of
\bot
with
p
; for an
\mathcal{L}
-formula
\varphi
with
x
,
y
, and
z
as the only its variables, let

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\varphi^{+}_{p}&=&\displaystyle\forall x\forall y\forall z\,\bigwedge\limits_{\mathclap{\psi\in\mathop{\mathit{sub}}\varphi}}(p\to\psi^{p})\to\varphi^{p}.\end{array}

It follows from
[
50
, Proposition 10.1]
that if
\varphi
does not contain
p
, then

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\varphi\in\mathbf{QInt}&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\varphi^{+}_{p}\in\mathbf{QInt}.\end{array}

(5.2)

Let
S_{6}
be a formula substitution defined by
S_{6}(P(x_{1},x_{2}))=(Q(x_{1})\wedge Q(x_{2})\to p)\vee q
, where
Q
is a unary predicate letter and
q
a proposition letter different from
p
.

Lemma 5.1

If
n\in\mathbb{X}
, then
S_{6}((K((S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n})^{+}))^{+}_{p})\in\mathbf{QInt}
.

Observe that

\begin{array}[]{lclll}n\in\mathbb{X}&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n}\hfill\in\parbox{23.54153pt}{$\mathbf{QCl}$}&&\mbox{(by Lemma\penalty\ \ref{lem:Trakhtenbrot:lem1:sib:binP})}\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&(S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n})^{+}\hfill\in\parbox{23.54153pt}{$\mathbf{QCl}$}&&\mbox{(by Corollary\penalty\ \ref{cor3:lem:false})}\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&K((S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n})^{+})\hfill\in\mathbf{QInt}&&\mbox{(by\penalty\ (\ref{eq:kolmogorov:1}))}\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&(K((S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n})^{+}))^{+}_{p}\hfill\in\mathbf{QInt}&&\mbox{(by\penalty\ (\ref{eq:positivization:1}))}\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&S_{6}((K((S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n})^{+}))^{+}_{p})\hfill\in\mathbf{QInt}&&\mbox{(since $S_{6}$ is a substitution).}\\
\end{array}

We say that a class
\mathscr{C}
of intuitionistic Kripke frames satisfies the
special weak Kripke–Hughes–Cresswell condition
(for short,
swKHC
) if, for every
n\in\mathds{N}
, there exists a Kripke frame
\langle W,R\rangle\in\mathscr{C}
with
w\in W
such that
R(w)
contains an
R
-antichain with
n
elements. Obviously, if a class of Kripke frames satisfies swKHC, then it also satisfies wKHC. However, if, for some
k\in\mathds{N}^{+}
, a class
\mathscr{C}
contains only Kripke frames of width
19
19

19

The greatest cardinality of antichains in the frame.
at most
k
, then it can not satisfy swKHC; at the same time, even the class of all linear Kripke frames (i.e., of width
1
) satisfies wKHC.

Lemma 5.2

Let
L
be a superintuitionistic predicate logic such that
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is an swKHC class. Then,
n\in\mathbb{Y}
implies

S_{6}((K((S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n})^{+}))^{+}_{p})\not\in\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{c}}_{\mathit{dfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Y}
. By Lemma
3.17
, there exists a finite model
\bm{M}=\langle\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}\rangle
such that
\bm{M}\models\bm{sib}
and
\bm{M}\not\models S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n}
. Then, by Corollary
3.30
,
\bm{M}\not\models(S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n})^{+}
.

Since
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is an swKHC class, it contains an intuitionistic Kripke frame
\mathfrak{F}=\langle W,R\rangle
with a world
w_{0}
such that
R(w_{0})
contains an
R
-antichain with
|\mathcal{D}|^{2}
elements. Let

\begin{array}[]{lcl}W^{\prime}&=&\{w_{ab}:a,b\in\mathcal{D}\}\end{array}

be a subset of the antichain with
w_{ab}\neq w_{cd}
whenever
\{a,b\}\neq\{c,d\}
. Let
\mathfrak{M}=\langle\mathfrak{F}\odot\mathcal{D},I\rangle
be an intuitionistic model such that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}p&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&R(w)\cap W^{\prime}=\varnothing;\\
\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}q&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&w_{0}\not\in R(w);\\
\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}Q(a)&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\mbox{either $\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}p$}\\
&&\mbox{or, for some $b\in\mathcal{D}$, both $w\in\{w_{ab},w_{ba}\}$ and $\bm{M}\not\models P(a,b)$.}\end{array}

Sublemma 5.3

Let
\varphi
be a positive formula that contains no predicate letters different from
P
. Then
\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}S_{6}((K(\varphi))^{p})
, for every
w\in W
such that
w_{0}\not\in R(w)
.

By induction on
\varphi
. Let
w
be a world of
\mathfrak{F}
such that
w_{0}\not\in R(w)
. If
\varphi=P(x,y)
, then
S_{6}((K(\varphi))^{p})=(((Q(x)\wedge Q(y)\to p)\vee q)\to p)\to p
. Assume that
\mathfrak{M},w\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi))^{p})
, for some assignment
g
. Then there exists
w^{\prime}\in R(w)
such that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}((Q(x)\wedge Q(y)\to p)\vee q)\to p&\mbox{and}&\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}p,\end{array}

which imply
\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}(Q(x)\wedge Q(y)\to p)\vee q
, that is impossible, since
\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}q
by the definition of
\mathfrak{M}
. Thus,
\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}S_{6}((K(\varphi))^{p})
. The induction step is straightforward and is left to the reader.

Sublemma 5.4

Let
\varphi
be a positive formula that contains no predicate letters different from
P
. Then, for every assignment
g
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi))^{p})&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\bm{M}\models^{g}\varphi.\end{array}

By induction on
\varphi
.

If
\varphi=P(x,y)
, then
S_{6}((K(\varphi))^{p})=(((Q(x)\wedge Q(y)\to p)\vee q)\to p)\to p
. Observe that, by the definition of
\mathfrak{M}
, for all
a,b\in\mathcal{D}
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(((Q(a)\wedge Q(b)\to p)\vee q)\to p)\to p&\mathrel{\Longleftrightarrow}&\bm{M}\not\models P(a,b),\end{array}

that provides us with the equivalence required, for
\varphi=P(x,y)
.

The cases
\varphi=\varphi^{\prime}\wedge\varphi^{\prime\prime}
,
\varphi=\varphi^{\prime}\vee\varphi^{\prime\prime}
,
\varphi=\varphi^{\prime}\to\varphi^{\prime\prime}
,
\varphi=\exists x\,\varphi^{\prime}
, and
\varphi=\forall x\,\varphi^{\prime}
are similar to each other; let us consider some of them.

Let
\varphi=\varphi^{\prime}\to\varphi^{\prime\prime}
. Then

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\bm{M}\not\models^{g}\varphi&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\mbox{$\bm{M}\models^{g}\varphi^{\prime}$ and $\bm{M}\not\models^{g}\varphi^{\prime\prime}$}\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\mbox{$\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime}))^{p})$ and $\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime\prime}))^{p})$}\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime}))^{p})\to S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime\prime}))^{p})\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime}))^{p}\to(K(\varphi^{\prime\prime}))^{p})\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}(S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime}))^{p}\to(K(\varphi^{\prime\prime}))^{p})\to p)\to p\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime}\to\varphi^{\prime\prime}))^{p}),\\
&\mbox{i.e.,}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi))^{p}).\end{array}

Suppose that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi))^{p})
, i.e.,
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime}\to\varphi^{\prime\prime}))^{p})
. Then there exists
w\in R(w_{0})
such that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime})\to(K(\varphi^{\prime\prime})))^{p})\to p&\mbox{and}&\mathfrak{M},w\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}p,\end{array}

which imply
\mathfrak{M},w\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime})\to(K(\varphi^{\prime\prime})))^{p})
. But then there exists
w^{\prime}\in R(w)
such that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime}))^{p})&\mbox{and}&\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime\prime}))^{p}).\end{array}

It then follows from Sublemma
5.3
, that
w_{0}\in R(w^{\prime})
. Since
R
is antisymmetric, from
w_{0}Rw^{\prime}
and
w^{\prime}Rw_{0}
we obtain
w^{\prime}=w_{0}
. Thus, by inductive hypothesis,

\bm{M}\models^{g}\varphi^{\prime}
and
\bm{M}\not\models^{g}\varphi^{\prime\prime}
, and hence,
\bm{M}\not\models^{g}\varphi^{\prime}\to\varphi^{\prime\prime}
, i.e.
\bm{M}\not\models^{g}\varphi
.

Let
\varphi=\forall x\,\varphi^{\prime}
. Then

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\bm{M}\not\models^{g}\varphi&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\mbox{$\bm{M}\not\models^{h}\varphi^{\prime}$, for some $h$ such that $h\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x}}{{=}}g$}\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{h}S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime}))^{p})\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{h}(S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime}))^{p})\to p)\to p\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\forall x\,\varphi^{\prime}))^{p}),\\
&\mbox{i.e.,}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi))^{p}).\end{array}

Suppose that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}((K(\varphi))^{p})
, i.e.,
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{h}(S_{6}(\forall x\,(K(\varphi^{\prime}))^{p})\to p)\to p
. Then there exists
w\in R(w_{0})
such that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}(\forall x\,(K(\varphi^{\prime}))^{p})\to p&\mbox{and}&\mathfrak{M},w0\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}p,\end{array}

which imply
\mathfrak{M},w\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{g}S_{6}(\forall x\,(K(\varphi^{\prime}))^{p})
. But then, for some
w^{\prime}\in R(w)
and an assignment
h
such that
h\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x}}{{=}}g
,

\begin{array}[]{c}\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}^{h}S_{6}((K(\varphi^{\prime}))^{p}).\end{array}

It then follows from Sublemma
5.3
, that
w_{0}\in R(w^{\prime})
, and therefore,
w^{\prime}=w_{0}
. Thus, by inductive hypothesis,
\bm{M}\models^{h}\varphi^{\prime}
, and hence,
\bm{M}\not\models^{g}\forall x\,\varphi^{\prime}
, i.e.
\bm{M}\not\models^{g}\varphi
.

All other cases are similar (even simpler) and are left to the reader.

Sublemma 5.5

If
\psi\in\mathop{\mathit{sub}}K((S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n})^{+})
, then
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}p\to S_{6}(\psi^{p})
.

It is sufficient to observe that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\forall x\,Q(x)
, for every
w\in W
such that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}p
.

Let us apply the observations obtained:

\begin{array}[]{lcll}\bm{M}\not\models(S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n})^{+}&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}S_{6}((K((S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n})^{+}))^{p})&\mbox{(by Sublemma\penalty\ \ref{sublem:2:lem:2:QInt:positive:3var})}\\
&\mathrel{\Longrightarrow}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}S_{6}((K((S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n})^{+}))^{+}_{p})&\mbox{(by Sublemma\penalty\ \ref{sublem:3:lem:2:QInt:positive:3var}).}\end{array}

Hence,
S_{6}((K((S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n})^{+}))^{+}_{p})\not\in\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{c}}_{\mathit{dfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
.

Lemma 5.6

Let
L
be a superintuitionistic predicate logic such that
\mathop{\mbox{{{fin}}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is an swKHC class. Then,
n\in\mathbb{Y}
implies

S_{6}((K(S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n}))^{+}_{p})\not\in\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{c}}_{\mathit{wfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
5.2
with the difference that the corresponding intuitionistic Kripke frame must be finite; such a frame exists in
\mathop{\mbox{{{fin}}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
, since it is an swKHC class.

Let us eliminate
p
and
q
. To this end, define a substitution
S_{7}
by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}S_{7}(p)&=&\forall x\,Q(x);\\
S_{7}(q)&=&\forall x\forall y\,((Q(x)\to Q(y))\vee(Q(y)\to Q(x))).\\
\end{array}

Lemma 5.7

If
n\in\mathbb{X}
, then
S_{7}S_{6}((K(S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n}))^{+}_{p})\in\mathbf{QInt}
.

Follows from Lemma
5.1
, since
S_{7}
is a substitution.

Lemma 5.8

Let
L
be a superintuitionistic predicate logic such that
\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is an swKHC class. Then,
n\in\mathbb{Y}
implies

S_{7}S_{6}((K(S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n}))^{+}_{p})\not\in\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{c}}_{\mathit{dfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
.

Follows from the proof of Lemma
5.2
, since, for model
\mathfrak{M}
constructed in the proof,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}p\leftrightarrow S_{7}(p)&\mbox{and}&\mathfrak{M}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}q\leftrightarrow S_{7}(q).\end{array}

Lemma 5.9

Let
L
be a superintuitionistic predicate logic such that
\mathop{\mbox{{{fin}}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
is an swKHC class. Then,
n\in\mathbb{Y}
implies

S_{7}S_{6}((K(S_{1}\textit{MTiling}^{\mathbb{X}}_{n}))^{+}_{p})\not\in\mathop{\mbox{{{QSIL}}}^{\mathit{c}}_{\mathit{wfin}}}\mathop{\mbox{{{KF}}}}L
.

Similarly, follows from the proof of Lemma
5.6
(with mentioned modifications of the proof of Lemma
5.2
); we leave the details to the reader.

For every
n\in\mathds{N}^{+}
, let
\bm{bd}_{n}
be the intuitionistic formula bounding the depth of intuitionistic Kripke frames by
n
, see
[
7
, Proposition 2.38]
. Let us make an observation.

Theorem 5.10

The positive fragments of logics

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbf{QInt}&\mbox{and\/}&\mathbf{QInt}_{\mathit{dfin}}\hfill+\bm{bd}_{2}+\bm{cd},\\
\mathbf{QInt}&\mbox{and\/}&\mathbf{QInt}_{\mathit{wfin}}\hfill+\bm{bd}_{2}+\bm{cd},\\
\mathbf{QInt}&\mbox{and\/}&\mathbf{QKC}_{\mathit{dfin}}\hfill+\bm{bd}_{3}+\bm{cd},\\
\mathbf{QInt}&\mbox{and\/}&\mathbf{QKC}_{\mathit{wfin}}\hfill+\bm{bd}_{3}+\bm{cd}\end{array}

are recursively inseparable in the language with a single unary predicate letter and three individual variables.

Follows from Lemmas
5.7
,
5.8
, and
5.9
.

Corollary 5.11

Let
\mathbf{QInt}\subseteq L\subseteq L^{\prime}
and also either
L^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbf{QInt}+\bm{bd}_{2}+\bm{cd}
or
L^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbf{QKC}+\bm{bd}_{3}+\bm{cd}
. Then the positive fragments of
L
and
L^{\prime}_{\mathit{wfin}}
as well as
L
and
L^{\prime}_{\mathit{dfin}}
are recursively inseparable in the language with a single unary predicate letter and three individual variables.

Corollary 5.12

Let
L
be one of
\mathbf{QInt}
,
\mathbf{QKP}
,
\mathbf{QKC}
,
\mathbf{QInt}+\bm{bd}_{n}
with
n\geqslant 2
,
\mathbf{QKC}+\bm{bd}_{n}
with
n\geqslant 3
. Then the positive fragments of
L
and
L_{\mathit{wfin}}
as well as
L
and
L_{\mathit{dfin}}
are recursively inseparable in the language with a single unary predicate letter and three individual variables.

5.3
Positive monadic fragments and two variables

To obtain similar results for the language with two individual variables, we have to modify the formulas describing, for every
n\in\mathds{N}
, the special
T_{n}
-tiling.

We shall use the formulas and denotations introduced in Section
3.4
. We define a formula similar to
\mathit{Tiling}^{\prime}_{n}
using just two individual variables. As in the modal case, let us use a new unary predicate letter
C
. Define (cf.
[
22
]
) the following
\mathcal{L}
-formulas:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{3}&=&\forall x\forall y\,(V^{\prime}_{n}(x,y)\wedge\exists x\,(C(x)\wedge H^{\prime}_{n}(y,x))\to\forall y\,(H^{\prime}_{n}(x,y)\to\forall x\,(C(x)\to V^{\prime}_{n}(y,x))));\\
\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{5}&=&\forall x\forall y\,(V^{\prime}_{n}(x,y)\vee\neg V^{\prime}_{n}(x,y)).\end{array}

Let, for convenience,

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{0}=\mathit{TC}_{0},&\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{1}=\mathit{TC}^{\prime}_{1},&\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{2}=\mathit{TC}^{\prime}_{2},&\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{4}=\mathit{TC}_{4}.\end{array}

Then, define

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathit{Tiling}^{\mathit{int}}_{n}&=&\displaystyle\bigwedge\limits_{\mathclap{i=0}}^{5}\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{i}\wedge\mathit{DSR}\wedge\mathit{DSU}.\end{array}

Finally, we define a new, intuitionistic, modification of
\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}
by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{M}^{\mathit{int}}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}&=&(\mathit{Tiling}^{\mathit{int}}_{n}\to\exists x\,P_{1}(x)\vee\exists x\,\neg C(x))^{+},\\
\end{array}

where
\varphi^{+}
is the formula obtained from
\varphi
by replacing
\bot
with
\mathit{false}=\forall x\forall y\,P(x,y)
, see Section
3.6
.

Lemma 5.13

If
n\in\mathbb{X}
, then
\mathit{M}^{\mathit{int}}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\in\mathbf{QInt}
.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
4.25
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{X}
. Suppose that
\mathit{M}^{\mathit{int}}\mathit{Tiling}_{n}^{\mathbb{X}}\not\in\mathbf{QInt}
. Then there exist an intuitionistic model
\mathfrak{M}=\langle W,R,D,I\rangle
and a world
w_{0}\in W
such that

\begin{array}[]{llcl}\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(\mathit{Tiling}^{\mathit{int}}_{n})^{+},&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\exists x\,P_{1}(x),&\mbox{and}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\exists x\,(C(x)\to\mathit{false}).\\
\end{array}

(5.3)

Next, we are going, for all
i,j\in\mathds{N}
, to pick out an element
a_{i}^{j}\in D_{w_{0}}
so that, for all
i,j\in\mathds{N}
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(H^{\prime}_{n}(a_{i}^{j},a_{i+1}^{j}))^{+}&\mbox{and}&\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(V^{\prime}_{n}(a_{i}^{j},a_{i}^{j+1}))^{+}.\end{array}

Since
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{4}
, there exists
a_{0}^{0}\in D_{w_{0}}
such that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}P_{0}(a_{0}^{0})
.

Let
k\in\mathds{N}
. Suppose, for all
i,j\in\{0,\ldots,k\}
, the element
a_{i}^{j}
is defined; we have to define, for all
i,j\in\{0,\ldots,k\}
, the elements
a_{k+1}^{j}
,
a_{i}^{k+1}
, and
a_{k+1}^{k+1}
.

Due to
(\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{2})^{+}
, there exists
b\in D_{w_{0}}
such that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(V^{\prime}_{n}(a_{0}^{k},b))^{+}
. Then take
a_{0}^{k+1}=b
. To define other elements, let us prove an auxiliary statement, cf.
[
22
]
.

Sublemma 5.14

Let
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(H^{\prime}_{n}(a,c))^{+}\wedge(V^{\prime}_{n}(a,e))^{+}\wedge(H^{\prime}_{n}(e,b))^{+}
, for some
a,c,e,b\in D_{w_{0}}
. Then
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(V^{\prime}_{n}(c,b))^{+}
.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
4.26
.

Assume that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(H^{\prime}(a,c))^{+}\wedge(V^{\prime}(a,e))^{+}\wedge(H^{\prime}_{n}(e,b))^{+}
.
Since
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\exists x\,(C(x)\to\mathit{false})
, there exists
w\in R(w_{0})
such that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}C(b)
and
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\not\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\mathit{false}
. By the heredity condition,
\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(H^{\prime}(a,c))^{+}\wedge(V^{\prime}(a,e))^{+}\wedge(H^{\prime}(e,b))^{+}
. Then we obtain by
(\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{3})^{+}
that
\mathfrak{M},w\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(V^{\prime}(c,b))^{+})
. Finally,
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(V^{\prime}(c,b))^{+}
follows by
(\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{5})^{+}
.

Suppose that
a_{0}^{k+1},\ldots,a_{i}^{k+1}
are already defined, for some
i\in\{0,\ldots,k\}
; we have to define
a_{i+1}^{k+1}
. Due to
(\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{1})^{+}
, there exists
b\in D_{w_{0}}
such that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(H^{\prime}_{n}(a_{i}^{k+1},b))^{+}
. Since also
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(H^{\prime}_{n}(a_{i}^{k},a_{i+1}^{k}))^{+}
and
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(V^{\prime}_{n}(a_{i}^{k},a_{i}^{k+1}))^{+}
, we obtain, by Sublemma
5.14
, that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(H^{\prime}_{n}(a_{i+1}^{k+1},b))^{+}
, and we can take
a_{i+1}^{k+1}=b
.

To define
a_{k+1}^{k+1}
, observe that, by
(\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{1})^{+}
, there exists
b\in D_{w_{0}}
such that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}H^{\prime}_{n}(a_{k}^{k+1},b)
; take
a_{k+1}^{k+1}=b
.

Suppose that
a_{k+1}^{k+1},\ldots,a_{k+1}^{j+1}
are already defined for some
j\in\{0,\ldots,k\}
; we have to define
a_{k+1}^{j}
. Due to
(\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{1})^{+}
, there exists
c\in D_{w_{0}}
such that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(H^{\prime}_{n}(a_{k}^{j},c))^{+}
. Then,

\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(H^{\prime}_{n}(a_{k}^{j},c))^{+}\wedge(V^{\prime}_{n}(a_{k}^{j},a_{k}^{j+1}))^{+}\wedge(H^{\prime}_{n}(a_{k}^{j+1},a_{k+1}^{j+1}))^{+},

and, by Sublemma
5.14
,
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(V^{\prime}_{n}(c,a_{k+1}^{j+1}))^{+}
. Hence, we may take
a_{k+1}^{k+1}=c
.

For all
i,j\in\mathds{N}
, due to
(\mathit{TC}^{\mathit{int}}_{0})^{+}
, there exists a unique
m\in\{0,\ldots,k_{n}\}
such that
\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\models P_{m}(a_{i}^{j})
. Then, put
f(i,j)=t^{n}_{m}
.
Then
f\colon\mathds{N}\times\mathds{N}\to T_{n}
is a
T_{n}
-tiling, since

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}(H_{n}(a_{i}^{j},a_{i+1}^{j}))^{+}&\Longrightarrow&\mathop{\hbox to5.52pt{\vbox to5.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}

{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.09 0 L 3.54 3.54 L 7.09 7.09}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}

\mathfrak{M},w_{0}\mathrel{|\hskip-1.25pt|\hskip-2.8pt{-}}\hfill(V_{n}(a_{i}^{j},a_{i}^{j+1}))^{+}&\Longrightarrow&\mathop{\hbox to5.52pt{\vbox to5.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
